Reflective Data Catalog - example usage#

Reflective’s unified Python interface for accessing SAI (Stratospheric Aerosol Injection) climate model data across cloud providers (S3, GCS, Azure, Cloudflare R2) for use on the Reflective Cloud Hub. Note: At this time, most non ESGF and ESM sources will not be accessible outside of the Reflective Cloud Hub due to technical limitations. We are working on storing the data in new locations and will update when it’s ready.

This is an Intake-like interface for browsing, searching, and loading SRM related datasets in a unified manner. All available datasets can be seen here.

# first, install with pip
%pip install reflective-data-catalog

Using the Reflective Data Catalog#

See what sources are available with:

rdc.list_sources()

================================================================================
AVAILABLE DATA SOURCES
================================================================================

All sources use the same interface:
  ds = catalog.source_name(param='value').to_dask()  # Lazy load
  ds = catalog.source_name(param='value').read()     # Load to memory

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
GOOGLE CLOUD CMIP6/GeoMIP (via catalog.esm / catalog.geomip_cloud)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  ESM Catalog (catalog.esm):
    catalog.esm.search(experiment_id=['G6sulfur','ssp245'], variable_id='tas', table_id='Amon')
    catalog.esm.load(experiment_id='G6sulfur', variable_id='tas')
    catalog.esm.list_experiments(activity_id='GeoMIP')
    catalog.esm.list_models(experiment_id='G6sulfur')
    catalog.esm.list_variables(experiment_id='G6sulfur')

  GeoMIP Cloud Helper (catalog.geomip_cloud):
    catalog.geomip_cloud.g6sulfur(variable='tas')
    catalog.geomip_cloud.g6solar(variable='tas')
    catalog.geomip_cloud.load_ensemble(experiments=['G6sulfur','ssp245','ssp585'])
    catalog.geomip_cloud.list_models()
    catalog.geomip_cloud.summary()

================================================================================
ESGF DATA SOURCES (via catalog.esgf)
================================================================================

GeoMIP Experiments (catalog.esgf.geomip):
  - g6sulfur(model='UKESM1-0-LL', variable='tas')
  - g6solar(model='UKESM1-0-LL', variable='tas')

CMIP6 SSP Scenarios (catalog.esgf.ssp):
  - ssp126/ssp245/ssp585(model='...', variable='...')

Direct search:
  catalog.esgf.search(project='CMIP6', experiment_id='...')

================================================================================

Search for data with

rdc.search(term='ssp245')
rdc.search(variable='tas')
rdc.search(tag='SAI')
rdc.search(term='ssp245', variable='tas')

Using with ESGF sources#

Search for ESGF data only

rdc.esgf.search(project='CMIP6',
                experiment_id='G6solar',
                source_id='UKESM1-0-LL',
                variable_id='tas'
               )

Load GEOMIP G6 Sulfar data#

geomip_g6Sulfur_ds = rdc.esgf.geomip.g6sulfur()
geomip_g6Sulfur_ds

Load a specific SSP#

ssp245_ds = rdc.esgf.ssp.ssp245()

It’s (rdc.esgf) intake-esgf under the hood and all functionality can still be used.

Using with ESM sources#

Search and load in one step

ds = rdc.esm.load(
    experiment_id=['G6sulfur', 'ssp245', 'ssp585'],
    variable_id='tas',
    table_id='Amon',
    require_all_on=['source_id', 'institution_id'],
)

or use the GeoMIP convience helper

ds = rdc.geomip_cloud.load_ensemble(
    experiments=['G6sulfur', 'ssp245', 'ssp585'],
    variable='tas',
)

Quickly load a single experiment

ds_dict = rdc.geomip_cloud.g6sulfur(variable='tas')

Explore what is available

rdc.geomip_cloud.list_models()
rdc.geomip_cloud.list_variables(experiment_id='G6sulfur')
rdc.geomip_cloud.summary()

direct search then load

subset = rdc.esm.search(
    experiment_id='G6sulfur',
    variable_id=['tas'],
    table_id='Amon',
)
datasets = subset.to_dataset_dict()

Just like with ESGF, this (rdc.esm) is intake-esm under the hood and all of it’s functionality is available to use.

Getting Help#

rdc.help()
